Easywurld — 30-day, 10-post-per-day content calendar

Monday 2 November–Tuesday 1 December 2026 · Africa/Lagos (WAT, UTC+1)

300 core posts · 900 platform placements · 10 core posts/day on TikTok, Instagram and Facebook.

Strategy before production

Active campaign: Monday 2 November–Tuesday 1 December 2026 Platforms: TikTok, Instagram and Facebook Volume: 10 core posts per day, adapted for each platform = 30 scheduled placements per day, 900 across 30 days. Timezone: Africa/Lagos (WAT, UTC+1)

This replaces the 22-post weekday plan. The owner wants a high-volume, series-led channel. The strategy below keeps that volume but makes it repeatable, recognisable and reviewable.

1. Is the idea good?

Yes — the series structure is the strongest part. It gives people recurring reasons to follow: niche ideas, myth-checking, trend analysis, AI workflows, humour, offer education, copywriting lessons and a daily introduction to Easywurld.

The risk is not the number 10. The risk is publishing 10 versions of the same generic tip, or 10 hard sells, every day. We avoid that by giving each slot a distinct job, rotating the offer, varying the format, and adapting the copy to each platform.

Rule: 10 core ideas per day, not 30 separate ideas. Each idea is produced once and adapted for TikTok, Instagram and Facebook. That is 30 placements, but one production system.

2. Positioning

Easywurld helps small businesses make the message, website, content and follow-up work together.

Audience: owner-led small businesses in Lagos and across Nigeria. Business objective: relevant WhatsApp enquiries, well-matched DIY pack sales, and scoped project briefs. Voice: plain, specific, useful, slightly witty. No fake client results, no inflated marketing promises.

The account should feel like a useful marketing studio thinking out loud: here is what we would post, here is what we would question, here is what we would automate, here is what we would steal from great copywriters, and here is who we are.

3. The 10 daily series

SlotTimeSeriesJobDefault format
107:00If I ran this accountShow niche-specific social media thinkingTalking head + carousel
208:30Marketing Myth CourtDebunk or qualify common marketing adviceTalking head
310:00Trend DeskWhat is trending / what trended last week, and whether a business should use itScreen recap / talking head
411:30AI Automation BuildOne practical AI or automation workflowScreen demo / workflow carousel
513:00Meme: owner lifeRelatable small-business humourOriginal static / short video
614:30Offer Spotlight AProblem + what the pack actually containsCarousel / talking head
716:00Steal This Copy MoveBreak down a copywriting expert’s principle and apply itCarousel / talking head
817:30Meme: marketing / AI / clientsRelatable industry humourOriginal static / short video
919:00Offer Spotlight BObjection, limitation or next step for the same daily offerTalking head / static
1020:30Meet EasywurldA new angle on who we are, what we do and who we serveRotating format

Platform offset: TikTok at the slot time, Instagram +10 minutes, Facebook +20 minutes. These are starting test times, not proven best times.

Series 1 — If I ran this account

Prompt: “If I was managing social media for a [niche] in Lagos, this is what I would post this week.”

Each episode gives three post ideas, one mistake to avoid and one first action. Rotate niches: fashion, food, beauty, fitness, property, education, events, logistics, retail, services. Do not pretend Easywurld manages these accounts. Say “if I ran” or “here is the plan I would test.”

Series 2 — Marketing Myth Court

Prompt: “Marketing myth: [claim]. Verdict: [true / false / it depends]. Here is the test.”

Do not debunk with another absolute. Use verdicts: mostly false, partly true, depends on the goal. Give a small test the owner can run. Examples: “post every day”, “hashtags are dead”, “you need a website before content”, “AI can replace strategy”, “more followers means more sales”.

Series 3 — Trend Desk

Prompt: “This week’s trend watch: [format]. Should a small business use it? Here is the Easywurld adaptation.”

Trend posts must be verified before recording. Use TikTok Creative Center, Instagram Reels feed, platform newsrooms and dated trend reports. Save the source URL, date checked and example link in the production sheet. Do not invent a trend or use an unlicensed song because it is trending.

For launch week, candidate formats reported in late October 2026 include Brand Vibe, Pick Your Emoji, Gold Star Behavior, interface-as-content and short proof Reels. These are candidates, not guaranteed current trends. Recheck on 1 November. 2 1 5

Series 4 — AI Automation Build

Prompt: “One AI workflow a small business can test: [workflow]. Input → AI draft → human check → next action.”

Show the boundary. AI can draft, classify, summarise or prepare. A human approves prices, promises, legal/health/finance answers and customer-sensitive replies. Use real screen recordings or clearly labelled concept demos. Do not imply a workflow is live if it is a sample.

Series 5 and 8 — Two memes

One owner-life meme and one marketing/AI/client meme per day. Original text-led graphics or short videos. No stolen meme templates with unclear rights, no fake customer screenshots, no punching down at customers. The caption can add the useful lesson.

Series 6 and 9 — Two offer posts, one daily offer

Feature one offer per day. Post A explains the problem and contents. Post B handles an objection, limitation or next step. Rotate the nine packs. Do not sell nine packs in one day.

Current source price: ₦5,000 per DIY pack. Confirm price, contents, payment and delivery before publishing. State what the pack does not do. Custom projects are separate.

Series 7 — Steal This Copy Move

Prompt: “Steal this copy move from [expert]: [principle]. Don’t steal their words. Steal the structure. Here is an original Easywurld example.”

Break down the technique, not long copyrighted ads. Use short examples, attribute the source, and create a new Lagos/small-business example. Rotate Ogilvy, Schwartz, Sugarman, Halbert, Hopkins, Caples, Wiebe, Harry Dry and others. Marketing Examples and Copyhackers are useful modern reference points. 1 1

Series 10 — Meet Easywurld

A new introduction every day, not the same bio repeated. Rotate: who we serve, what we do, how we work, what we don’t do, sample tour, process, AI policy, handover, pricing philosophy, how to brief us, what happens after you message, why we label samples, and what we are building.

4. Format mix

The series are the structure; the format changes inside them.

  • AI talking head: niche plans, myths, offer explanations, copy lessons, introductions.
  • Screen demo: AI workflows, trend recaps, sample tours.
  • Carousel: niche plans, checklists, offer contents, copy breakdowns.
  • Original static/meme: two daily meme slots and some offer/intro posts.
  • Short text video: fast myths, trend verdicts, owner-life jokes.

Do not make every slot a talking head. The feed needs visual variety. The avatar is a production tool, not the entire brand.

5. Platform adaptation

PlatformAdaptation
TikTokNative vertical video, spoken hook, fast first frame, captions, one CTA. Static memes can be photo posts or short text videos.
InstagramReel or carousel depending on the idea. Carousel copy can be more structured. Check crop and safe areas.
FacebookNative text + image/video or carousel. Caption can be slightly more conversational. Do not just paste TikTok text with hashtags.

One core idea can have three captions. The hook and CTA stay consistent; the wording and format fit the platform.

6. AI talking-head rules

The owner wants AI talking heads. Use an approved owner likeness and voice, or a licensed provider avatar. No verified owner photo/CV was found in the accessible repos. The fictional demo founder image must not be used as the owner.

TikTok requires disclosure for realistic AI-generated media, including a person portrayed saying something they did not say. Enable the platform AI setting and keep a visible label. 1

House style: chest-up, eye-level, natural light, restrained gestures, corrected phrase captions, one useful cutaway, no logo intro, no long end card. CapCut’s guidance supports meaningful jump cuts paired with reframes or cutaways rather than decorative transitions. 1

Use own/licensed audio or TikTok’s Commercial Music Library with the applicable rights. A trending personal-account sound is not automatically cleared for a brand. 1

7. Production system for 10/day

Batch by series, not by day.

  • Sunday: write/verify the week’s 70 core ideas. Record talking-head batches by series. Record screen demos. Design memes and carousels from templates.
  • Daily: assemble the 10 core posts, adapt captions, QA, schedule.
  • Weekly: review series performance, not just individual posts.

Suggested batch blocks:

  1. Niche plans: 7 episodes.
  2. Myth Court: 7 episodes.
  3. Trend Desk: research + 7 recaps.
  4. AI Build: 7 workflows.
  5. Memes: 14 originals.
  6. Offer: one pack, 14 posts.
  7. Copy Move: 7 lessons.
  8. Meet Easywurld: 7 angles.

This is heavy. Use templates, avatar batch generation, caption presets and a shared asset library. If a slot fails QA, hold it rather than publishing unreviewed AI. The volume target stays 10/day; quality control is not optional.

8. Scheduling

TikTok’s official scheduler announcement describes a 15-minute to 10-day window and says scheduled posts must be deleted and re-uploaded to change them. Check current availability in your account. 1

Meta Business Suite can schedule Facebook and Instagram, but limits and features vary by account and surface. Check the planner in your own account. Do not assume a CSV auto-publishes.

The CSV in this repo is a production/planning sheet. Every row starts Draft. Mark Scheduled only after the platform confirms it. No social account is connected here.

9. Measurement

Review by series after 7 days:

  • Which series earns saves/shares?
  • Which earns watch time/completion?
  • Which earns profile visits and WhatsApp keyword enquiries?
  • Which offer gets qualified purchases or project briefs?
  • Which meme gets shares without attracting the wrong audience?

Keep 10/day for the test, but change hooks, formats and angles based on evidence. Do not declare a winner from one post. Do not invent month-end results.

10. Trust rules

  • All portfolio work is concept/sample. Label it.
  • No fake testimonials, rankings, client results or campaign metrics.
  • Hypothetical businesses/chats are labelled example.
  • DIY packs are not custom projects.
  • Trend posts need a dated source check.
  • Copywriting posts attribute the principle and use original examples.
  • AI presenters/voices are disclosed.
  • Private CVs, identity sources, customer messages and payment details stay out of public Git.

11. Launch gates

  • [ ] Confirm handles, website URL, WhatsApp and offer fulfilment.
  • [ ] Supply approved owner likeness/voice or use non-avatar fallbacks.
  • [ ] Verify launch-week trends on 1 November.
  • [ ] Produce first 70 core posts / 210 platform adaptations.
  • [ ] QA captions, prices, disclosures, crops and audio rights.
  • [ ] Schedule only approved assets.
  • [ ] Record actual URLs and results afterwards.

Series playbook

Active campaign: 2 November–1 December 2026 Cadence: 10 core posts per day, adapted to TikTok, Instagram and Facebook = 30 placements per day.

This playbook turns the strategy into repeatable production rules. The canonical editorial source is marketing/series-plan.json. The expanded machine-readable plan is marketing/content-plan.json.

1. Daily slot map

SlotWATSeriesCore formatJob

2. Series rules

If I ran this account

  • Say “If I ran” or “I would test.” Do not imply Easywurld manages the business.
  • Give three post ideas, one mistake and one first action.
  • Use Lagos/Nigeria context where relevant, but do not invent local facts.
  • Label hypothetical businesses, prices and chats as examples.

Marketing Myth Court

  • Use a verdict: true, mostly false, partly true, false, or it depends.
  • Do not replace one absolute with another.
  • Give a small test the owner can run.
  • Do not claim platform rules without a current source.

Trend Desk

Trend posts are held until the verification log is complete.

  1. Check the dated source already attached to the brief.
  2. Within seven days before publishing, find a current public example on the target platform.
  3. Record the example URL, platform, date checked, rights check, relevance note and reviewer in marketing/TREND_VERIFICATION.csv.
  4. If the format is still relevant and rights are clear, update the script to say “this week” or “last week” only when the evidence supports it.
  5. If no current example is verified, publish only as a dated teardown: “Reported on [date]. Here is the structure.” Remove live-trend wording.

Late-October 2026 candidate formats include Brand Vibe, Pick Your Emoji, Gold Star Behavior, short proof Reels, native-interface tutorials and comment-keyword DM flows. These are candidates, not guaranteed November trends. 1 2 1

Do not use a trending personal-account sound for a brand unless the rights are cleared. TikTok Business Accounts should use the Commercial Music Library or separately licensed audio. 2

AI Automation Build

  • Show one workflow: input → AI draft → human check → output.
  • State what is not automated.
  • Use anonymised or fictional input.
  • Keep prices, promises, legal/medical/financial answers and complaints behind human review.
  • If using a repo demo, label it concept/sample. Do not imply it sent real messages or produced live results.

Memes

  • Two per day: one owner-life, one marketing/AI/client.
  • Original text-led graphics or short text videos.
  • Joke about the work, not the customer.
  • No fake notifications, stolen templates, private screenshots or punching down.
  • The caption carries the useful lesson.

Offer Spotlight A and B

  • One featured offer per day.
  • Post A: problem + actual contents + price + DIY boundary.
  • Post B: one objection or limitation + direct answer + same keyword CTA.
  • Rotate the nine packs. Do not sell all nine in one day.
  • Confirm price, contents, payment and delivery before publishing.
  • No guaranteed results, fake scarcity or invented testimonials.

Steal This Copy Move

  • Attribute the principle to the expert and source.
  • Steal the structure, not long copyrighted copy.
  • Use an original Easywurld/Lagos example.
  • Do not imply the expert endorses Easywurld.

Rotation: David Ogilvy, Eugene Schwartz, Joe Sugarman, Gary Halbert, Claude Hopkins, John Caples, Joanna Wiebe, Harry Dry, Ann Handley, Robert Bly.

Reference points: Ogilvy on research and headlines; Schwartz on existing desire and awareness; Sugarman on reading momentum; Halbert on market and offer; Hopkins on testing; Caples on self-interest headlines; Wiebe on voice-of-customer; Dry on concrete, checkable copy; Handley on utility and voice; Bly on benefits and clear CTAs. 1 1 2 1 1 1

Meet Easywurld

  • A new angle and format each day.
  • Rotate: studio map, one-sentence studio, process, sample labels, service menu, FAQ, AI policy, handover, pricing boundary, brief template, contact route, audience map, sample tour, series map, website anatomy, follow-up flow, local checklist, proposal anatomy, message worksheet, launch checklist, tool stack, behind the calendar, homepage tour, boundaries, who we serve, how to start, review rhythm, invitation, dictionary.
  • Do not invent team size, office, clients or results.
  • Do not use the fictional demo founder as the owner.

5. Weekly batch rhythm

Each week produces 70 core posts and 210 platform placements.

BlockWeekly outputNotes
Niche plans7 carouselsBatch by niche; use owned/labelled visuals
Myth Court7 talking headsRecord in one avatar/voice session
Trend Desk7 teardownsResearch first; hold until verified
AI Build7 workflowsRecord real demos or set workflow cards
Memes14 originalsDesign from one template system
Offer14 postsOne pack, two angles per day
Copy Move7 breakdownsAttribute principle, write original example
Meet Easywurld7 introsRotate format and angle

Suggested weekly timing:

  • Sunday: verify trends, approve scripts, batch record/edit, design statics.
  • Daily: assemble the 10 core posts, adapt captions, QA, schedule only approved assets.
  • Saturday/Sunday: reply, review series metrics, prepare next batch.

This is a heavy cadence. Use templates, batch generation and a shared asset library. If a slot fails QA, hold it rather than publishing unreviewed AI. Do not stack catch-up posts into a spam burst.

6. QA gate per placement

Before a placement moves from Draft to Scheduled:

  • [ ] Facts, price, offer and sample labels checked.
  • [ ] Trend verification complete, or live-trend wording removed.
  • [ ] Approved likeness/voice or non-avatar fallback.
  • [ ] Captions, pronunciation, crop and disclosure checked.
  • [ ] One CTA and destination tested.
  • [ ] Watched/read on a phone; human approval recorded.

Statuses: Draft → Script approved → Asset produced → QA approved → Scheduled → Published → Reviewed.

7. Measurement

Review by series after seven days:

  • Saves/shares for teaching and copy posts.
  • Watch time/completion for talking heads and demos.
  • Profile visits and WhatsApp keyword enquiries.
  • Qualified project briefs and confirmed pack sales.
  • Meme shares without attracting the wrong audience.

Keep the 10/day test, but change hooks, formats and angles based on evidence. Do not declare a winner from one post. Do not invent month-end results.

EW-D01-S02 · Myth Court: Posting every day guarantees growth.

Hook / first frame

Marketing myth: Posting every day guarantees growth.

Exact spoken script

Marketing myth: Posting every day guarantees growth Verdict: It depends. Frequency can create more chances to learn, but it does not fix an unclear offer or weak content. Ten useful series posts are different from ten random posts. Test it: Compare one week of consistent series posts with the previous week using saves, profile visits and relevant enquiries. That is Myth Court: keep the useful part, drop the absolute.

Edit / shot order

  • 0–3s: face + myth on screen.
  • 3–12s: verdict: It depends..
  • 12–28s: explain the nuance with one labelled example.
  • 28–38s: show the test: Compare one week of consistent series posts with the previous week using saves, profile visits and relevant enquiries.
  • 38–42s: one CTA.

EW-D16-S02 · Myth Court: Logo first, message later.

Hook / first frame

Marketing myth: Logo first, message later.

Exact spoken script

Marketing myth: Logo first, message later Verdict: Mostly false. A logo matters, but customers first need to understand what you do, who it is for and what happens next. Clarity can precede polish. Test it: Write the one-sentence offer before redesigning the mark. That is Myth Court: keep the useful part, drop the absolute.
